<a id="person-1"></a>
## 1. Burek Jozsef Moldovan Katz

**Identity:** https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Moldovan_Katz-1

**Known before this pass:** Name: Burek Jozsef Moldovan Katz; birth: c.1790 (tree estimate; no birth record inspected); death: c.1837, Bréb (tree estimate; original death entry uninspected)

**Finding and confidence:** Existing identity; baseline retained. Retain the family/source qualifications below.

**Birth:** c.1790 (tree estimate; no birth record inspected)

**Death:** c.1837, Bréb (tree estimate; original death entry uninspected)

**Descendant-line parent:** Root

**Named children:** Chaim Arye Leb Moldovan Kahan

**Spouse claims:** Rifke Berkovics (Moldovan Katz), b. 1800

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** Parents of Chaim Arye Leb Moldovan Kahan. Their other children are not shown; this tree follows Chaim Arye Leb's descendants.

MyHeritage review, 16 October 2026: Geni import 816884227 names Rifke Berkovics and son Chaim Arye Leb, but supplies no parents or vital dates for Jozsef. This repeats the linked Geni tree; it is not independent proof of parentage.

Historical context checked 16 October 2026: Magyar-Zsidó Oklevéltár VII (Budapest, 1963), printed pp.136, 306, 747, records eighteenth-century Jewish residents/counts at Gyulafalva/Gullfalva and Brééb. The 1728 Joszip Isak entry predates this Jozsef’s estimated birth by about 62 years. No identity or ancestral relationship is established; these early households remain unattached. See the bilingual research notes for transcription, column meanings and exact PDF pages.

Sefer Marmarosh (1983), printed p.66: the 1830 Breb census list includes Moldawan Yosif with 5 household members. The supplied image explicitly says the figures include all persons (כוללים כל הנפשות), so this is not evidence of five children; the number of children is unknown. This may be our Jozsef, but the identification remains provisional because no age, parents or names of other household members are given. The same list includes Moldawan Shlomo, 6 persons, and Moldawan Hers, 6. The original 1830 census has not been inspected.

18 October 2026, earlier Bréb community context only: the published 1768 census lists Moska Moszkovics with seven household persons including wife and children (volume XVI, document 22/2, printed p. 101 / PDF p. 103). Wife and children are unnamed. No relationship to Jozsef or continuity with the 1746 household has been established; Moska is not assigned as an ancestor.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: . These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

**Sources**

- [WikiTree Moldovan_Katz-1](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Moldovan_Katz-1)
- [WikiTree Berkovics-21](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Berkovics-21)
- [MyHeritage: Geni import (secondary tree)](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-40000/geni-world-family-tree?itemId=816884227&action=showRecord)
- [Magyar-Zsidó Oklevéltár VII: historical context, not established ancestry](https://real-eod.mtak.hu/2973/1/Magyar_zsido_okleveltar_VII.pdf#page=138)
- [Sefer Marmarosh 1983, p.66: 1830 Breb candidate household](https://archive.org/details/nybc314151/page/n225/mode/1up)
- [Bréb 1768: earlier community context; no family link established](https://kisebbsegkutato.tk.elte.hu/uploads/files/olvasoszoba/magyarzsidookleveltar/Magyar_zsido_okleveltar_XVI.pdf#page=103)
- [Geni profile reviewed 20 October 2026 (secondary family-tree claims)](https://www.geni.com/people/Burek-Jozsef-Moldovan-Katz/6000000223716886821)

## 2. Chaim Arye Leb Moldovan Kahan

**Identity:** https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Moldovan_Kahan-1

**Known before this pass:** Name: Chaim Arye Leb Moldovan Kahan; birth: 1827-06-24, Bréb, Máramaros, Hungary; death: 1897-06-24, Gyulafalu, Máramaros, Hungary

**Finding and confidence:** Existing identity; baseline retained. Retain the family/source qualifications below.

**Birth:** 1827-06-24, Bréb, Máramaros, Hungary

**Death:** 1897-06-24, Gyulafalu, Máramaros, Hungary

**Descendant-line parent:** Burek Jozsef Moldovan Katz

**Named children:** Moses Laser Moldovan Kahan; David Hers Moldavan Kahan; Dwora Kahan; Abraham Wolf Kahan; Fischel Moldovan Kahan; Dov Ber Bernat Kahan; Riwke Regina Kahan (Junger); Majer Jakob Kahan; Ruchel Kahan (Fogel); Frimet Kahan

**Spouse claims:** Wife recorded in 1848 (name unresolved; age 22; alive 1848); Freide Klein (birth year uncertain; estimated c.1850)

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** His death record (24 Jun 1897, innkeeper, aged 72, son of Jozsef Kahan and Rifke Berkovics, reported by son-in-law Sztrul Junger) is in the Máramaros Jewish Records Indexing Project (JewishGen Hungary), sheet Deaths 1895–1898, row 1897-76. Wives audit 15 October 2026: the original 1848 Gyulafalva census, household 6, records Leb age 22, wife age 22 (born approximately 1826), and child Mojse age 1. Her name is unresolved. Later birth citations name Freide for Fischel (1867), Riwke (1874), Majer Jakob (1877), Ruchel (1880) and Frimet (1883); Klein is named in Majer’s retrospective death and Ruchel’s marriage. Evidence points to two wives, but no remarriage/death record establishes the transition or exact lifetime total. Freide’s c.1850 birth is an unverified WikiTree estimate. Using Chaim’s listed 1827 birth, his ages at the last three births were 50, 53 and 56. Dwora’s 1858 mother name is cut off (…ter); no third wife is established. Dov/Bernat’s mother remains unknown. The spouse list does not assign a mother to every child. Earlier wife’s chronology: alive at the 1848 census; Freide documented as mother by Fischel’s birth, 28 December 1867. If the earlier union ended through widowhood, a death after the census and before the later union by 1867 is possible. The records do not exclude divorce and therefore do not establish a death year or interval.

MyHeritage review, 16 October 2026: the earlier-wife question was already known, not a new discovery. The wife aged 22 in 1848 would have been about 57 in 1883. The imported Geni records simultaneously estimate Freide born c.1850 and David Hersch born c.1855 and assign her as his mother; these claims are chronologically incompatible. David belonging to the earlier marriage is a hypothesis requiring a record naming his mother. Neither the birth span nor this faulty tree establishes the first wife’s identity or death.

The birth-index citation is Hajim, 24 June 1827, Bréb, father Burek / Joszip Moldovan, mother Rifke, Book 1271, 02-11; the original birth entry remains uninspected. Death index 1897-76 gives age 72 (implying about 1825) and a different birthplace, while the 1848 census age 22 implies about 1826. Those discrepancies remain unresolved; the exact birth date is an indexed claim, not an inspected original.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: June 24, 1827 Breb, Ocna Sugatag, MM, Romania; death: June 24, 1897 (70) Giulești, Giulesti, MM, Romania. These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

MyHeritage continuation, 17 October 2026: collection 20262, record 98414-, reproduces the already-known 1848 child Mojse, 1, with Leb and a wife indexed as Ne Afa. This derivative index does not identify the later adult or resolve his mother’s personal name. The Baden 1906 Dr. Moses Laser Kahane / Anna Deutscher marriage (file 1906, no.13) remains an unattached index candidate. The inspected 1905 Moses Kahan passenger original gives a family-level son/brother relationship to Bernet, not proof that our Moses and Bernat were brothers. Adult identity and sibling status remain unresolved.

18–19 October 2026 continuation: the newly checked Claims Conference namesakes Mojse Kahan (20231/98862-, age 17) and Mozes Moldovan (20231/101723-, age 20) are not this adult born about 1847. The 1886 New York and 1896 Quebec passenger candidates remain unattached pending original-image and household comparison; no adult death or new relative is established.

20 October 2026, WikiTree biography review: identifying Malka Malcsi Kahan, wife of Leb Mendelson, with Malke Moldovan born on 9 August 1871 to Moses and Reise is explicitly described by the biography as an uncertain assumption without a directly linking document. The biography also names Chaim Arye Leb and Freide Klein as alternative possible parents; they are not adopted here. Its estimate around 1873–1874 comes from maternal ages in her children’s cited birth entries. Its assumed death around 1903 comes from a daughter sharing her name, not an inspected death record. The earlier 1873/1903 values are now displayed as estimates/inferences with these qualifications; the exact 1871 birth is not imported. The existing Moses parent edge is marked possible, not proved, and no child is moved. The public API mother field is unlinked; that missing link does not cancel the mothers named in biography citations. This pass read profile text and index transcriptions, not the underlying register images.

**Spouse claims:** David Hersh Ganz (1888 – 1944)

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** MyHeritage Geni import 746159080 lists Chaja Ganz as another daughter, and Rosa Rachel Lebovici plus Rosa Lebovici as separate daughters. The two Rosa records share birth 7 April 1920, death 5 October 2014, matching parents and matching spouse names, making duplication likely; no extra Rosa node was added. Chaja is a candidate requiring supporting evidence, not an established new child.

17 October 2026: her son Yaakov’s original photographed headstone explicitly names his parents Frimid and David (BillionGraves 20557023, photo 14560429). This independently corroborates the existing son/parents relationship. Daughter Freida Reif’s matching SSA index also names mother Freida Kahan, though its birthplace conflicts with the tree; see her profile. No new child is inferred from copied member-tree sibling lists.

19 October 2026: Rochelle Leibovici’s original mother form, 1131015, supports the Frimith née Kahan / Dovid Hash Ganz household, but its fields are misplaced: KAHAN is in the first-name line and FRIMITH in the mother line. Birth is written 1883 and “Szeged, Roumania”, conflicting with the retained 10 June 1884/Bréb profile claim; the place is preserved as written, not silently changed to Sighet. Auschwitz/gas chamber is recorded, with no death year on that form. Berel age 17 is listed in its deceased-children section. Record 1298319 is another scan/copy of this same maternal statement, not an independent Berel testimony. A marginal sister-Chaya/Paris/Swiss-border account mentions a husband and two children, but the sister’s generation is ambiguous; no relative or family was attached from it. The father form 1298321 names Dovid Hersch, 1888, parents Srul Yaakov/Esther and wife Frimith née Kahan, corroborating the household without independently naming Frimith’s parents.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: June 10, 1884 Breb, Ocna Sugatag, MM, Romania; death: 1944 (59-60) Oświęcim, Distretto di Oświęcim, Voivodato della Piccola Polonia, Poland (Holocoast). The separate Quebec civil death index records death on 5 January 1958 in Montreal, birth year 1922, supporting the updated spouse label. Civil marital status Divorcé conflicts with later obituary wording; no divorce or additional spouse is inferred. The already-known obituary is also accessible through MyHeritage and mentions Brahm, Sandra, Jay, Ava and Khloe, but intermediate parentage is unspecified; no descendant edges were invented.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: November 01, 1924 Romania; death: September 27, 2014 (89) Montreal, Montreal, Quebec, Canada. ## 39. Mozses Iczik Kahan

**Identity:** https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahan-420

**Known before this pass:** Name: Mozses Iczik Kahan; birth: 1887-12-09, Bréb, Máramaros, Hungary; death: 1944, Auschwitz Concentration Camp, Oświęcim, Biała County, Kraków, Poland

**Finding and confidence:** Existing identity; baseline retained. Retain the family/source qualifications below.

**Birth:** 1887-12-09, Bréb, Máramaros, Hungary

**Death:** 1944, Auschwitz Concentration Camp, Oświęcim, Biała County, Kraków, Poland

**Descendant-line parent:** David Hers Moldavan Kahan

**Named children:** Chaim Leb Kahan (Lajos); Raziel Kahan; Frida Kahan (Weisberg); Gizi Gitsa Kahan; Jozsef Kahan

**Spouse claims:** Eszter Kahan (1892 – 1935)

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** A Yad Vashem forced-labor index names Lajos Kahan, born 1911, mother Eszter née Kahan, as missing. Chaim Leb’s linked profile gives birth in 1912. A possible civil/Hebrew name pairing is a search lead, not proof that Lajos and Leib identify the same man. Birth-year conflict and absent paternal identification must be resolved before attaching that record as established evidence. Jozsef Kahan, born 26 June 1914 to Eszter née Kahan, remains a possible son; his father is not named in the inspected index. Missing in a wartime list does not establish death in 1944.

Original Page of Testimony 3619896, inspected 19 October 2026: Frida Kohen Veisberg explicitly identifies herself as his daughter (signed 1 July 1999). She names Moshe Yitzchak Kohen, father David Tzvi Kohen, mother Reiza, wife Ester née Kohen, and EIGHT children. Birth is approximately 1892, while age at death is 55; these estimates conflict internally and with the profile’s 9 December 1887. The existing birth date is retained as a profile claim pending the original register. The testimony records deportation to the Berbești ghetto and death at Auschwitz in 1944. Four named children are already linked with established household identification; Jozsef remains provisional. Eight reported children therefore leave four not securely identified, or three if Jozsef belongs. No missing names or maternal assignments were invented. The children count is one daughter’s testimony, not eight individually identified child records.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: December 09, 1887 Breb, Ocna Sugatag, MM, Romania; death: 1944 (56-57) Oświęcim, Oświęcim County, Lillepolen, Poland (Holocaust ). These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

## 43. Frida Kahan (Weisberg)

**Identity:** https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahan-425

**Known before this pass:** Name: Frida Kahan (Weisberg); birth: 1925, Gyulafalu, Máramaros, Hungary; death: 2017-09-08, Haifa, Haifa, Israel

**Finding and confidence:** Existing identity; baseline retained. Retain the family/source qualifications below.

**Birth:** 1925, Gyulafalu, Máramaros, Hungary

**Death:** 2017-09-08, Haifa, Haifa, Israel

**Descendant-line parent:** Mozses Iczik Kahan

**Named children:** Moshe Yitzhak Weisberg

**Spouse claims:** Shimon Weisberg (Simon; c.1923 – ?)

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** Her husband Shimon Weisberg was born in Hungary around 1923 (he was 21 when the Germans entered Budapest in 1944), the second-youngest of 11 children. He survived the war in Budapest, sailed from Italy on the "Four Freedoms", reached Haifa on 3 Aug 1946 and was held in the Atlit camp, then settled in Kiryat Shmuel (Haifa). After years of trying for children, their son Moshe was born in 1956. Shimon and Frida both died at a good old age. Source: "Shimon Weisberg, a true survivor", written by their son Moshe Yitzhak Weisberg for the Bridge Between Generations project, 2025 (https://www.ravdori.co.il/stories/%D7%A9%D7%9E%D7%A2%D7%95%D7%9F-%D7%95%D7%99%D7%A1%D7%91%D7%A8%D7%92-%D7%A9%D7%95%D7%A8%D7%93-%D7%90%D7%9E%D7%99%D7%AA%D7%99/). Possible Geni profile for Shimon, not confirmed (different birth year): https://www.geni.com/people/Shimon-Weissberg/6000000007537493746

17 October 2026: MyHeritage member-tree search results repeat parents Moses Yitzhak and Esther and list Alte Feige Kahan among siblings. This is an unverified candidate additional sister; some copies also duplicate Chaim Leb. No original or independent identifying record was inspected, so no sibling was attached. A separate BillionGraves search returned other identities. The next DFS search, son Moshe Yitzhak Weisberg (1956), was blocked by the daily limit.

<a id="person-61"></a>
## 61. Dov Ber Bernat Kahan

**Identity:** https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahan-376

**Known before this pass:** Name: Dov Ber Bernat Kahan; birth: Estimated c. 1868; recorded ages imply c. 1863–1872, Giuleşti, Maramureş, Romania; death: 23 January 1928 — proposed identification with a surname-less JOWBR entry; gravestone uninspected

**Finding and confidence:** Existing identity; updated born. Relationship remains provisional.

**Birth:** 1868, Giulești, Giulesti, MM, Romania (Geni-reported; unverified)

**Death:** 23 January 1928 — proposed identification with a surname-less JOWBR entry; gravestone uninspected

**Descendant-line parent:** Chaim Arye Leb Moldovan Kahan

**Named children:** Jente Kahan; Brana Kahan; Hane Kahan; Ziszel Kahan (Muscal); Chaim Arye Leb Kahana; Feige Kahan (Adler); Reiza Kahan (Wieder); Majer Jakab Kahan; Abraham Lieber Kahan; Freide Kahan (Herskovits); Shimon Menachem Kahan / מנחם כהן; Yosef Mordechai Kahan

**Spouse claims:** Rivka Rifke Regina Liberman (1871 – 1944)

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** Birth estimated around 1868; recorded ages vary and no birth entry has been identified. His parentage remains a supported but unresolved indirect argument. The surname-less JOWBR entry J_MARAMURE_0008659 reads Dov son of Chaim Aryeh HaKohen, age 56, Rosh Chodesh Shevat 5688. No stone image was available; identifying this grave with our Dov and its father with the specific Chaim Arye Leb is not established. The Junger in-law links in Brana’s death 1898-10 and Leb’s death 1897-76 support investigation but require identity confirmation. Witnessing Ruchel’s marriage 1901-26 or reporting Majer’s death 1902-229 is not an explicit sibling relationship. Freide Klein is not established as his mother. Brotherhood with Moses Laser and David Hers remains conditional on their respective parentage.

Original brother testimony 1501887 directly pairs Dov with Bernat and Rivka with Reghina. Son Menachem’s testimony 1625122 directly identifies Rifka KAHAN / רבקה כהן, née Lieberman, as his mother. The latter has a blank husband field and does not name Dov’s father. Multiple names/index entries derived from one testimony are one source.

Current roster: twelve children, five sons and seven daughters, including infant Brana. Six linked JewishGen birth-index citations explicitly name parents and sex: Jente, Brana, Hane, Ziszel, Leb and Majer Jakab. Other connections use the linked testimony, memorial and family evidence; this is not twelve independently verified birth records. Hillel confirms Meir Yaakov = Majer Jakab, with 21 March 1905 retained, and Yosef Mordechai as Chaim Arye’s brother and a survivor. Yosef is supported by family testimony; an independent identifying record remains unlocated. Excluding Brana leaves five sons and six daughters. Sefer Marmarosh’s five-son/five-daughter count therefore remains unresolved. Its father-name David conflicts with original Dov/Bernat evidence; a typo is plausible but unproved.

The inspected Be’er Chaim memorial names the author’s mother Rivka as wife of Dov Kahana and explicitly identifies Liber, Feiga, Freida and Alta Yenta as the author’s siblings. It does not establish Dov’s own parents. MyHeritage’s Geni import repeats proposed ancestry rather than independently proving it. Exact index references and the earlier search history remain in the research ledger.

19 October 2026, original family-count check: Rivka’s two Pages of Testimony each report TEN children. Son Menachem’s 1625122 gives age 72, parents Shimon Menachem/Tzipora, Sat-Sugatag, and Auschwitz in Sivan 1944; grandson Dov Kahana’s 3983900, signed 2 July 2000, gives approximate birth 1880, age 65, widow of Dov, Auschwitz and approximately June 1944. These age/birth estimates conflict with each other and with the mother’s recorded age 32 in Chaim’s 1903 civil birth. Ten also differs from the twelve father-linked children currently displayed under Dov, and the five-son/five-daughter book count remains unresolved. A father’s spouse list does not prove every linked child had that mother. The testimonies give no complete named roster; no child is deleted or reassigned to force agreement, and no second mother is invented.

20 October 2026: the Geni media image was compared with Rivka’s already-inspected Page of Testimony 3983900, signed by grandson Dov on 2 July 2000. It is a copy of that same statement, not a gravestone or an independent witness. It adds no new family fact or support for Dov’s proposed parentage. The linked Geni document page loaded, but its external JOWBR request returned HTTP 403; that route supplied no fresh cemetery content and cannot establish that an inscription or image is absent. Existing dates, relationships and uncertainty remain unchanged.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: 1868 Giulești, Giulesti, MM, Romania; death: January 23, 1928 (59-60) Giulești, Giulesti, MM, Romania. These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

**Identity:** https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahan-379

**Finding and confidence:** Existing identity; baseline retained. Retain the family/source qualifications below.

**Birth:** 1894-02-07, Gyulafalu, Máramaros, Hungary

**Death:** 1944, Auschwitz (family testimony, Hillel, 18 October 2026)

**Descendant-line parent:** Dov Ber Bernat Kahan

**Named children:** Lipot Aryeh Lieberman; Frida (Freide) Lieberman; Naftali Nandor Lieberman; Margit Lieberman

**Spouse claims:** Jakov Jankel Liberman (1892 – 1944)

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** Research 15 Oct 2026: Lipot’s photographed Holocaust memorial names his mother Alta Yenta daughter of Berl, supporting this connection to Dov/Ber alongside the 1894 birth index and Be’er Chaim memorial. The linked Geni profile instead names Avraham as her father; this conflict remains recorded. The memorial names her among relatives killed in Auschwitz in 1944; it does not name the siblings individually.

The inspected Be’er Chaim memorial names his sister Alta Yenta. A separate line commemorates the author’s mother Rivka, wife of Dov Kahana. These are separate statements, not a verbatim quotation naming this person as “child of Dov and Rivka”. Identification with this profile uses the other linked family evidence. The memorial gives no individual birth date, death date or death place. Timișoara in the author’s biography does not establish this relative’s residence.

Hillel confirmed on 18 October 2026 that Alta Yenta (Jente) died at Auschwitz in 1944. This is family testimony, not an original civil death record. Lipot’s previously inspected family memorial names his mother Alta Yenta daughter of Berl and commemorates his parents and siblings murdered at Auschwitz in 1944. The Be’er Chaim memorial separately names Alta Yenta among the author’s sisters who were Shoah victims; it supplies no individual death date or place. Birth 7 February 1894 is retained as an indexed claim.

19 October 2026: Hillel named daughter Margit Lieberman, added from explicit family testimony. The previous one-unidentified-child slot may correspond to Margit; it is no longer displayed as an additional fifth child. Four children are now named in this tree, including provisional Frida. Private profile fields remain unavailable, so this is not a verified complete named roster.

Geni access update, 19 October 2026: the initial CAPTCHA cleared. Jente’s public page shows Jacov as husband, four masked child slots, and timeline births for Lipot (1914), Frida (1917) and Naftali (1919). The private child profile supplied for Margit explicitly lists Jente/Jacov and those three siblings; Hillel supplies the name Margit. This corroborates the household as secondary tree evidence, not an original register. Geni’s Avraham-father assertion remains contrary to the separately cited Bernat/Dov evidence.

Geni-led source review, 19 October 2026: the attached JewishGen birth-index image names Jente, female, 7 February 1894, father KAHAN Bernat and mother LIBERMAN Rifke, both of Gyulafalu; Romanian National Archive, Baia Mare branch, Book 1101, entry 37-01. This supports the existing indexed birth/parentage claim but is a transcription screenshot, not the original register. The separate Geni Yente Kahan profile 6000000184192334884 names Bernat/Rivka; the Jente Lieberman profile 6000000016589578823 instead names Avraham. No online merge is inferred from that discrepancy; the existing household identification also relies on the separately cited family memorial and family testimony.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: February 07, 1894 Giulești, Giulesti, MM, Romania. These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: . These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

**Identity:** https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahana-151

**Finding and confidence:** Existing identity; updated died, years. Retain the family/source qualifications below.

**Birth:** 10 April 1903, Maragyulafalu, Kingdom of Hungary (now Giulești, Romania); inspected civil birth entry

**Death:** 1956 (family-confirmed year; exact date unresolved)

**Descendant-line parent:** Dov Ber Bernat Kahan

**Named children:** Penina Perl Kahana (Miller); Dov Kahana; Shalom Kahana

**Spouse claims:** Leah Kahana (c.1907 – bef.1983; sister of Ezra and Ienta)

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** Hillel confirmed two additional children, distinct from Penina, Dov and Shalom, who died before 1943. Their names, sex and birth dates remain unknown; they are represented by two unnamed historical child slots, not invented named nodes. At 14:04 Hillel explicitly corrected the death year to 1956, which is the current family-confirmed field. If the previously reported day/month 2 Sivan is retained, Hebcal gives 12 May 1956 / 2 Sivan 5716. That is a conditional calendar reconstruction, not a confirmed exact date. The earlier 2 Sivan 5717 statement would give 1 June 1957 and conflicts with the latest correction; 1957 is not accepted as the death year. Earlier 23 May 1956 and 26 March 1956 profile claims remain source history. The inspected 10 April 1903 birth remains unchanged; no original death record is claimed.

Earlier research notes, before the 13:56 family corrections:

Family relationships use the linked records and Hillel’s identified family testimony; named living relatives are not all independently documented. The inspected Be’er Chaim memorial lists the author’s parents-in-law Yitzhak Aizik Miller and Henya, followed by their daughter Yenta Zefir and son Ezra. Ezra and Yenta are therefore siblings-in-law, not children of the author and Leah. Yad Vashem 10991960 / 10991958 indexes them under Chaim Arye and Leah; the original layout contradicts that assignment. The book does not name the author’s two reportedly deceased children.

The title page identifies Rabbi Chaim Arye Kahana, rabbi in Jaffa and formerly Timișoara, as the deceased author. His surviving writings were edited by his son-in-law and sons and published in Bnei Brak in 5748 (1987–1988). This is a posthumous publication, not evidence that he lived until 1988.

Family memory says two of the rabbi's children died in Timișoara; they have not been found yet. Per his grandson's Wikipedia article he headed the Nadvorna Hasidim's "Maamar Mordechai" yeshiva in Tel Aviv and was a community rabbi in Jaffa in the early 1950s; he was a student of Rabbi Yehuda Segal Rosner of Székelyhíd (author of Imrei Yehuda). https://he.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D7%9E%D7%90%D7%99%D7%A8_%D7%9B%D7%94%D7%A0%D7%90_%28%D7%93%D7%99%D7%99%D7%9F%29 Original civil birth entry inspected 14 October 2026: Leb born 10 April 1903 in Maragyulafalu, registered 16 April at Bardfalva; father Kahan Bernad/Bernat, age 35, born and resident Maragyulafalu; mother Liberman Regina, age 32, born Falusugatag and resident Maragyulafalu. Archive MM-F-00743-1-1-00236, former Book 35, entry 64, folio 176v, part 3 PDF page 57. Children research found specific Timisoara Jewish register references but no additional proven child; see the ledger. Ferenc and Rozalia Kahan burial-index entries lack age and parents and remain unattached.

MyHeritage Geni import 472131443 gives 26 February 1903 at Sighet, conflicting with the already inspected original Leb birth, 10 April 1903 at Maragyulafalu. The original-record date and place are retained. This discrepancy needs identity/event reconciliation, not replacement with the imported tree.

Sefer Marmarosh (1983), p.62, identifies Chaim Leib as Timisoara dayan, eldest son and father-in-law of Rabbi Miller of Nazareth Illit; it names his brother Mendel. These combined details strongly identify this family. It says all five brothers studied with Yehuda Rosner at Szekelyhid. Its death date, 2 Sivan 5717, conflicts with the current 23 May 1956 field and remains an unresolved book claim. Its father-name David also conflicts with Bernad/Dov in original evidence. Dates and parentage were not overwritten.

20 October 2026: the current Geni profile reports death on 26 March 1956. This adds a third unresolved claim alongside the earlier profile date 23 May 1956 and Sefer Marmarosh’s 2 Sivan 5717. No alternative is selected as verified. Geni’s 26 February 1903/Sighet birth repeats the already documented conflict; the inspected original 10 April 1903 birth at Maragyulafalu remains unchanged. He remains Dov/Bernat’s son and Reiza’s brother; a native profile roster does not reassign those relationships.

20 October 2026, child-count clarification: the current Geni roster has four profile entries: Penina, Shalom and two Dov records. Whether the two Dov profiles represent one person or two remains unresolved. The three existing named children are retained; the former fixed one-unnamed-child counter is omitted because the extra source profile does not establish a distinct fourth child. Earlier family recollection of two children who died in Timișoara remains a separate unresolved claim, not a replacement count.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: February 26, 1903 Sighetu Marmației, Județul Maramureș, Romania; death: March 26, 1956 (53) Israel. These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

Miller’s appointment in Nazareth Illit from 1961 is a later event and does not date their marriage or imply her father was then alive. She wrote the memoir הרב שלי: יומנה של רבנית (My Rabbi: A Rebbetzin's Diary, Sifriat Maariv, 1986). Source: https://he.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D7%A4%D7%A0%D7%97%D7%A1_%D7%9E%D7%99%D7%9C%D7%A8 Original immigrant list: arrived 17 November 1960 aboard T. Herzl (Theodor Herzl), with her husband Friedrich (Pinchas) Miller and two daughters; destination Academics’ Hotel, Nazareth. Israel State Archives GL-15501/6, scan 84/175, printed page 2, rows 89–92; heading on scan 83. Penina is recorded as born 1929, her husband 1919, parents Asher / Esther; immigrant certificate 150204. High-confidence household identification; her parent names are not repeated in her row. Birthplace supplied by Hillel Kahana on 14 October 2026: Sighetu Marmației, also the birthplace of the other sibling (Penina/Dov). This is family testimony; the original birth entry remains unlocated.

MyHeritage comparison, 16 October 2026: the Greenblatt tree incorrectly assigns Penina (1929), Dov (1930) and Shalom (1943) to Kalonymos Kalman Kahana (1816–1899) and Rivka Hartstein-Rappoport (1827–1870). Both proposed parents died before all three births. This mixed-generation assignment is rejected; it does not establish ancestry. Existing family and arrival-record evidence identifying Chaim Arye and Leah remains in place.

19 October 2026, Geni-led named-child update: Asher Miller is now shown provisionally from the inspected child and reciprocal parent profiles, with Pinchas/Penina explicitly named. At the 19 October checkpoint, two sibling slots were still unnamed in this tree; saved named-profile evidence now supports the two displayed daughters provisionally, alongside Asher. The 1960 arrival sheet’s two daughters are not individually identified with those private profiles from a count alone. Geni’s exact 1 January 1929 birth and 23 September 1988 death are displayed as unverified profile claims; no original birth or death record has been inspected. Pinchas’s Geni death 6 March 1993 conflicts with the retained 1992 biographical claim; the three inspected family-book attachments do not resolve that date or independently name modern Asher as his son.

20 October 2026: Geni’s own profile fields report 1 January 1929 and 23 September 1988. The exact day/month values remain unverified; no original birth or death record was obtained in this pass. The Sighet birthplace remains attributed to Hillel’s family testimony, while Geni gives Romania. That earlier date-only checkpoint retained Asher and two unnamed slots. The current tree displays Asher, Menucha and Rivka from the separately reviewed saved profiles; these collaborative-tree identifications remain provisional.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: January 01, 1929 Romania; death: September 23, 1988 (59). These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

<a id="person-119"></a>
## 119. Shalom Kahana

**Identity:** https://www.geni.com/people/Shalom-Kahana/6000000009318607641

**Known before this pass:** Name: Shalom Kahana; birth: 1943-02-01 (Geni-reported; unverified), Timișoara, Romania (family memorial); death: 1996-03-30 (Geni-reported; unverified)

**Finding and confidence:** Existing identity; baseline retained. Retain the family/source qualifications below.

**Birth:** 1943-02-01 (Geni-reported; unverified), Timișoara, Romania (family memorial)

**Death:** 1996-03-30 (Geni-reported; unverified)

**Descendant-line parent:** Chaim Arye Leb Kahana

**Named children:** Chaim Aryeh Kahana (Haim Arie); Michal Kahana (Tikochinsky); Yael Adoram; Yedidia Kahana; Nachshon Kahana; Elisheva Shezifi; Ishai Kahana; Hillel Kahana; Avia Prinz; Akiva Kahana; Gad Kahana; Lea Eyal; Iska Glazer; Rachel Kahana

**Spouse claims:** Ruth Pasternak (Kahana; רות פסטרנק)

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** Hillel rejected the extra Sara child claim as a mistake on 20 October 2026. Sara is not added to this household. The confirmed Hillel, Ishai and Nachshon source aliases do not create additional siblings; the fourteen family-confirmed children remain.

Earlier research notes, before the 13:56 family corrections:

Arrived with his parents on the ship Transylvania (טרנסילבניה) on 1 Feb 1951; the immigrant list spells the family "כהן": "Cohen Leib, b. 1903, parents Dov/Rivka", wife Leah b. 1907 and son Shalom b. 1943, immigrant no. 918?0, sent to Sha'ar HaAliya (ש.ע.) (Israel State Archives, immigrant lists Jan–Feb 1951, GL-15496/1, p. 167, rows 480–482: https://journaliststudio.google.com/pinpoint/document-view?collection=585158a3cc8e02af&p=1&docid=ec7469b5b0ffa001_585158a3cc8e02af&dapvm=2 ). Family memorial: born in Timișoara to Rabbi Chaim Arye Kahana; married Ruth; studied chemistry at Bar-Ilan and taught at Nehalim before becoming principal of HaRo’eh High School in Ramat Gan. The memorial publishes his ethical will, dated Av 5755 (1995). Family testimony from Hillel on 14 October 2026 identifies 14 children: the thirteen named in the supplied order, plus Rachel (1972–1972). Ruth née Pasternak is the mother of all fourteen; her parents are Eliezer and Erna (their surnames were not separately provided). The earlier Geni-derived count of 18 is superseded by this family testimony. All fourteen children are now named, but their descendant branches remain incomplete. See the research notes for sources and outstanding family details. Original 1951 arrival page re-inspected 14 October 2026: Shalom, born 1943, appears in the household group immediately below Cohen Leib (1903; parents Dov/Rivka) and Leah (1907). His own parent-name cell is blank; the grouping supports the household identification rather than being a separate birth record.

MyHeritage comparison, 16 October 2026: the Greenblatt tree incorrectly assigns Penina (1929), Dov (1930) and Shalom (1943) to Kalonymos Kalman Kahana (1816–1899) and Rivka Hartstein-Rappoport (1827–1870). Both proposed parents died before all three births. This mixed-generation assignment is rejected; it does not establish ancestry. Existing family and arrival-record evidence identifying Chaim Arye and Leah remains in place.

20 October 2026: the current Geni profile reports birth on 1 February 1943 and death on 30 March 1996. These exact dates are reported profile claims, not independently inspected birth or death records. The earlier family-memorial birthplace and original 1951 arrival evidence remain unchanged. Geni displays eighteen child-profile links, including unresolved duplicate/candidate records; the fourteen children confirmed by Hillel remain the canonical roster until those identities are reconciled. The renewed full descendant review includes Haim Arie’s branch and supersedes its earlier temporary deferral.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: February 01, 1943 Timishiuara, Romania; death: March 30, 1996 (53). These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

<a id="person-170"></a>
## 170. Feige Kahan (Adler)

**Identity:** https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahan-395

**Known before this pass:** Name: Feige Kahan (Adler); birth: c.1904 (age 40 in husband’s 1944 Holocaust testimony; exact birth unverified); death: unknown

**Finding and confidence:** Existing identity; updated born. Retain the family/source qualifications below.

**Birth:** circa 1904 (Geni-reported; unverified)

**Death:** Unknown / not supplied

**Descendant-line parent:** Dov Ber Bernat Kahan

**Named children:** Dov Adler; Judit Adler; Eszter Adler

**Spouse claims:** Eizik Nuszen Adler (1904 – 1944)

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** Her husband Eizik Nuszen Adler was the son of Ziszel Kahan (b. 1867, WikiTree Kahan-399), whose parents are unknown — possibly another Kahan relative. Her husband Eizik Nuszen Adler was born 14 Feb 1904 in Máragyulafalu to Nuchem Adler (of Disznópatak) and Ziszel Kahan. Source: Máramaros Jewish Records Indexing Project (JewishGen Hungary), sheet Births 1904–1905, row 1904-34. Her brother Menachem ("Menakhem Kohen", brother-in-law) filed a page of testimony for her husband: "Nathan Ytzkhak (Aizik) Adler", b. 1902 in Giulești to Nachum and Zissel, merchant, murdered in Auschwitz in 1944 (https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/1859762). Original husband’s testimony inspected 15 Oct 2026: Feiga Kohen age 40 and seven children; named children Dov age 14, Yehudit age 14, Ester age 11. Four children remain unnamed. Shared reported age 14 is not proof of twins; Feige’s and Reiza’s estimated 1904 birth years are not proof they were twins. Husband’s 1902 testimony birth differs from the 1904 civil birth index.

The inspected Be’er Chaim memorial names his sister Feiga. A separate line commemorates the author’s mother Rivka, wife of Dov Kahana. These are separate statements, not a verbatim quotation naming this person as “child of Dov and Rivka”. Identification with this profile uses the other linked family evidence. The memorial gives no individual birth date, death date or death place. Timișoara in the author’s biography does not establish this relative’s residence.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: circa 1904; death: 1944 (35-44). These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

<a id="person-174"></a>
## 174. Reiza Kahan (Wieder)

**Identity:** https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahan-407

**Known before this pass:** Name: Reiza Kahan (Wieder); birth: c.1904 (testimony-derived estimate); conflicting Geni date: 11 April 1898, unverified; death: 1944, Auschwitz Concentration Camp, Oświęcim, Biała County, Kraków, Poland

**Finding and confidence:** Existing identity; baseline retained. Retain the family/source qualifications below.

**Birth:** c.1904 (testimony-derived estimate); conflicting Geni date: 11 April 1898, unverified

**Death:** 1944, Auschwitz Concentration Camp, Oświęcim, Biała County, Kraków, Poland

**Descendant-line parent:** Dov Ber Bernat Kahan

**Named children:** Rakhel Wieder; Mirl Wieder; David Wieder; Tzipora Wieder; Yaakov Tzvi Wieder; Menachem Mendel Wieder

**Spouse claims:** Avraham Wieder (birth estimates 1895–1900, Deșești; died 1944)

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** Recorded in Yad Vashem as "Reiza née Kohen", wife of Abraham Wieder, living in Deșești; her children's records are the source for Rakhel, Mirl and David. A probable match for her husband: Avraham Vider, b. 1896 in Deșești to Yaakov Tzvi and Brana Rivka, merchant, "married to Rakhel", murdered in Auschwitz in 1944. The page was filed by his niece Shoshana Ovich (https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/1171294). His mother Brana Rivka was born in Crăciunești to Khona and Reiza Rakhel (https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/810885). The wife's name "Rakhel" differs from Reiza, so this is a likely match, not a certain one. Original-testimony audit 15 Oct 2026: grouped Yad Vashem 13777487 includes item 811147, Menachem Kohen as brother-in-law, explicitly names wife Reiza Kohen daughter of Dov and seven children. Item 671693, nephew Meir Zikherman, independently names Reiza Kohen and four murdered children: Tzipora, David, Yaakov Tzvi and Menachem Mendel. Menachem’s sheet also names Rachel. Added the three names absent from the baseline. The tree displays six names, but five are securely legible in these originals; Mirl’s derived entry is provisional. One or two of the reported seven remain unidentified, depending on that reading. Husband birth estimates differ (1895, 1896, 1900); the repeated parents and spouse support identity, but the exact birth year remains unresolved.

MyHeritage Geni import 189753962 gives birth 11 April 1898 at Sat-Șugatag, whereas the baseline testimony-derived estimate is c.1904. The imported exact date has not been independently verified and does not replace the existing estimate.

Originals re-read 19 October 2026: 671693 lists Tzipora, David, Yaakov Tzvi and the corrected/crossed fourth-row reading Menachem Mendel; 811147 securely adds Rachel. The Mirl index 8846874 derives from that same 671693 image and has no independently legible separate row in this reading. Mirl is therefore retained as a provisional indexed person, without deleting or merging her. Five children are securely named in the combined originals; with seven reported, one or two remain unidentified depending on Mirl. A word in the left child-table cell of 811147 is not safely adopted as an individual camp/place. Child ages and death dates remain unrecorded; the parent’s Auschwitz/1944 field is not copied to them.

Geni-attached sources read directly 19 October 2026: niece Shoshana Ovitz’s original testimony, signed 2 September 1999, is the already-known 1171294 statement. It gives Avraham Vider, birth 1896, age 48, parents Yaakov Tzvi/Brana, wife Rachel, and a child count written “7–8”; it supplies no child names. Wife Rachel versus Reiza and the seven-versus-seven-to-eight count remain explicit differences, not an invented second marriage or an extra child. The other two Geni testimony attachments repeat the already-read Meir/Menachem originals and add no independent named child. A separate JewishGen birth-index screenshot gives Abraham, male, 10 March 1896, Desze, father VIDER Jakobb of Hernecs age 42, mother FEIG Brane of Karacsonyfálva age 28; Inventory 1371, Book 45, entry 1896-40. That transcription supports Geni’s precise birth claim but is not an original register; identification with the husband and the 1895/1900 testimonial conflict remain qualified. The Geni child profiles name Tzipora, Rachel, Yaakov Tzvi and Menachem; they do not independently resolve the corrected fourth-row handwriting or a separate Mirl. No relative, eighth child or exact parental birth year is added.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: April 11, 1898 Sat-Șugatag, Maramureș County, Romania; death: June 1944 (46) Oświęcim, Oświęcim County, Lesser Poland Voivodeship, Poland (Murdered by the Nazis). These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

<a id="person-181"></a>
## 181. Majer Jakab Kahan

**Identity:** https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahan-382

**Known before this pass:** Name: Majer Jakab Kahan; birth: 1905-03-21, Gyulafalu, Máramaros, Hungary; death: unknown

**Finding and confidence:** Existing identity; baseline retained. Retain the family/source qualifications below.

**Birth:** 1905-03-21, Gyulafalu, Máramaros, Hungary

**Death:** Unknown / not supplied

**Descendant-line parent:** Dov Ber Bernat Kahan

**Named children:** Dov Kahana; Tzippora Kahana (Neuman)

**Spouse claims:** Chaya Roza Indig (c.1910 – ?; survived)

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** His birth record notes he married Roza Indig in Giulești on 27 Mar 1937. Source: Máramaros Jewish Records Indexing Project (JewishGen Hungary), sheet Births 1904–1905, row 1905-72. On Geni he is "Meir Yakob Kahana (Mayer Yakov), c.1905", husband of Chaya Roza Indig (c.1910), daughter of Shlomo Zalman Gedalia Indig. They had two children: Dov (1937–2019) and Tzippora (b. 1939) (https://www.geni.com/people/Meir-Kahana/6000000002683182314). Geni names his father "Dov Heller". The father-name/surname conflict is unresolved; it must not be dismissed as a data-entry slip without evidence. Roza survived the Shoah: Yad Vashem lists "Roza Khaia Indig Kahana" of Giulești as alive after the war, on a page of testimony for her family (https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/16399576). Her father Shlomo Zalman Gedalia Indig (b. 1880, Nyagova; merchant in Giulești) was murdered in Auschwitz (https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/13832093). His survival is not documented.

Hillel confirmed on 18 October 2026 that Meir Yaakov and Majer Jakab are the same person and requested 21 March 1905 as the retained birth date. The basis is JewishGen birth-index entry 1905-72, Inventory 1373, Book 36. The uncited Ancestry-derived 21 May date is not adopted. This confirmation concerns the names; the separate Geni father-surname conflict remains documented.

19 October 2026, wife’s birth-family sources: Chaya Roza Kahana explicitly signs as DAUGHTER on her parents’ original pages 1838943 and 1902561, dated 10 July 1957, corroborating her postwar survival. Both report SEVEN children in the Indig parents’ household; this is not seven children of Majer/Meir. Another daughter-witness Miriam Gros writes בת on 1838927 (the index wrongly says son), and Meir Gros writes חתן, son-in-law, on 1846707 (index wrongly says daughter-in-law); the latter does not identify which daughter was his wife. Mindl Glazer signs as daughter on Berta’s 1837545. The apparent Seni Brana/Berta Bodrits variants and birth estimates 1886/1887 are preserved. Father Zalman’s parents conflict: Israel Arye/Chaya Roza on 1902561 versus Elimelech/Chaya Reiza on 1838927. No earlier ancestor, sibling marriage or extra child is assigned to reconcile those statements.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: May 21, 1905. These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: circa 1905 Romania. These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

**Birth:** 1911, Giulești (reported in brother’s testimony); conflicting tree date: 3 September 1908

**Death:** 1944; Hungarian labour-camp context in original testimony; exact place unresolved

**Descendant-line parent:** Dov Ber Bernat Kahan

**Named children:** Beniamin Kahan; Dov Kahan

**Spouse claims:** Kreindel Shreiber

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** Original Page of Testimony 1501887, filed by Menachem Kohen as his brother, names father Dov / Bernat, mother Rivka / Reghina, and wife Kreindel Schreiber. Hillel identifies the submitter as Shimon Menachem Kahan. It reports birth in 1911 at Giulești, residence in Săcueni, and death in 1944. Five children are reported; only Dov and Beniamin are named, leaving three unidentified. Their individual fates are not inferred from the number.

The MyHeritage Geni import instead reports birth on 3 September 1908. That conflicting exact date is unverified; 1911 remains the testimony-reported year.

The inspected Be’er Chaim memorial explicitly names the author’s brother Liber. A separate line names the author’s mother Rivka, wife of Dov Kahana. The former sentence “Liber Kahana, child of Dov and Rivka” was my reconstruction, incorrectly presented as a quotation. The book does not supply Abraham as an additional name, a birth year or an individual death place. Identifying its Liber with Abraham Lieber uses the independent brother’s testimony. Săcueni’s community deportation history alone does not establish his individual death place.

The title page identifies Rabbi Chaim Arye Kahana, rabbi in Jaffa and formerly Timișoara, as the deceased author. His surviving writings were edited by his son-in-law and sons and published in Bnei Brak in 5748 (1987–1988). This is a posthumous publication, not evidence that he lived until 1988.

19 October 2026: an enlarged re-reading of original 1501887 clearly mentions a Hungarian labour camp in the death/circumstances field; the exact camp and parts of the handwritten sentence remain unresolved. It must not be replaced with Auschwitz from community history or another relative. The recorded year 1944 remains; no exact day/month is adopted. Wife Kreindel Schreiber, age 30, five children and named sons Dov/Beniamin were already known. No individual child death place/date is supplied by the blank child cells.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: September 03, 1908; death: 1944 (35-36). These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

Exact Gregorian death date unresolved. Grave: ה / ד / 10 / 1.

**Finding and confidence:** Existing identity; updated born. Retain the family/source qualifications below.

**Birth:** 1913 (Geni-reported; unverified)

**Death:** 28 Tevet 5733; burial 2 January 1973, Netanya. Exact Gregorian death date unresolved. Grave: ה / ד / 10 / 1.

**Descendant-line parent:** Dov Ber Bernat Kahan

**Named children:** Frida Kahan; Tova Blima Kahan

**Spouse claims:** Frida Frimed Berkovitz (1916 – 1944)

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** Hillel identifies him as his grandfather Chaim Arye’s brother. In original testimony 1625122, Menachem explicitly identifies Rifka KAHAN / רבקה כהן, née Lieberman, as his mother. In testimony 1501887 he calls himself Abraham Lieber’s brother and names father Dov / Bernat and mother Rivka / Reghina. Identifying this submitter with the full Shimon Menachem profile also uses Hillel’s family identification.

His wife’s original testimony 1600529, signed 12 January 1956, identifies Menachem as husband of Frimed Frida Berkovits and names daughters Frida (1940) and Tova Bluma (1942). USC interview 15743 indexes Henyah Fishkovits’s biological sister Fridah Kahan; the spoken segments have not been transcribed.

The official Netanya screenshot gives burial on 2 January 1973 and Hebrew death date 28 Tevet 5733, father Dov, block ה, section ד, row 10, place 1. It does not explicitly give the same Gregorian death date. Sefer Marmarosh p.62 reports death on 1 January 1973; an evening death could explain the difference, but no time has been established. The imported 1974 date conflicts with both and is rejected.

Birth remains unresolved: profile c.1913, biography c.1900, and an uninspected age-70 gravestone citation implying c.1902–1903. The burial register does not supply a birth year.

20 October 2026 Geni source review: birth: 1913; death: January 02, 1974 (60-61) Netanya, HaSharon, Center District, Israel. These are attributed profile claims, not independently inspected originals. Existing original/family evidence and all baseline children are retained; a source profile omitting a spouse or children does not establish their absence.

**Evidence, qualifications and remaining work:** Shoah victim: perished at Mauthausen concentration camp on 24 February 1945, according to the inspected original death registration. Identification with this family profile remains probable. His birth record notes he married Etel Davidovici in Tășnad (Sălaj county) on 27 Jan 1931. Source: Máramaros Jewish Records Indexing Project (JewishGen Hungary), sheet Births 1895–1897, row 1896-217.

17 October 2026: probable identification in an inspected original death-register image: Mozes Junger, born 17 December 1896 in Maragyulafalva, died 24 FEBRUARY 1945 at 04:15 in Mauthausen. Exact birth date and town match the baseline; father, mother and spouse fields are blank, so the parental connection is not independently proved by this document. MyHeritage 11007/2762220- incorrectly indexes 24 MARCH 1945; the image takes precedence. This is a retrospective Arolsen registration dated 4 July 1956, certificate 5244, Hessisches Staatsarchiv Marburg, HStAMR Best. 926 Nr. 393, Sterbenebenregister 1956, S. 51 (MyHeritage image 45/400; file 00051.jpg). The crossed-out printed unmarried formula does not establish marital status and does not contradict the previously indexed 1931 marriage.
